### 2. **Tier-Aware Placeholder with Market Cap Classification**

```python
# Market cap data (simplified - in production you'd fetch from database)
market_caps = {
    "GOSS": 0.3,    # $300M
    "IMCR": 2.5,    # $2.5B
    "INCY": 12.0,   # $12B
    "KYMR": 1.8,    # $1.8B
    "ACLX": 2.1,    # $2.1B
    "CELC": 0.5,    # $500M
    "RYTM": 1.2,    # $1.2B
    "RNA": 1.5,     # $1.5B
    "IONS": 4.8,    # $4.8B
    "BBIO": 1.9,    # $1.9B
    "UTHR": 11.0,   # $11B
    "ASND": 0.8,    # $800M
    "MDGL": 1.6,    # $1.6B
    "CDTX": 0.6,    # $600M
    "CMPX": 0.4,    # $400M
    "EPRX": 0.3,    # $300M
    "ARGX": 2.8,    # $2.8B
    "AMLX": 0.2,    # $200M
    "VTRS": 12.5,   # $12.5B
    "CNC": 8.2,     # $8.2B
    "SOLV": 0.4,    # $400M
    "NUVL": 0.3,    # $300M
    "JAZZ": 9.1,    # $9.1B
    "CLS": 0.6,     # $600M
}

def classify_tiers(market_caps: Dict[str, float]) -> Dict[str, str]:
    """Classify tickers into tiers based on market cap."""
    tiers = {}
    for ticker, cap in market_caps.items():
        if cap >= 5.0:
            tiers[ticker] = "Tier 1 (Large Cap)"
        elif cap >= 1.0:
            tiers[ticker] = "Tier 2 (Mid Cap)"
        else:
            tiers[ticker] = "Tier 3 (Small Cap)"
    return tiers

def apply_tier_aware_normalization(scores: Dict[str, float], tiers: Dict[str, str]) -> Dict[str, float]:
    """Apply tier-specific normalization."""
    # Group by tier
    tier_scores = {}
    for ticker, tier in tiers.items():
        if tier not in tier_scores:
            tier_scores[tier] = []
        tier_scores[tier].append((ticker, scores[ticker]))
    
    # Normalize within each tier
    normalized = {}
    for tier, ticker_scores in tier_scores.items():
        tier_values = [score for _, score in ticker_scores]
        min_val = min(tier_values)
        max_val = max(tier_values)
        range_val = max_val - min_val
        
        for ticker, score in ticker_scores:
            if range_val > 0:
                # Min-max normalization within tier
                normalized[ticker] = (score - min_val) / range_val * 100
            else:
                normalized[ticker] = 50.0  # Middle if all same
    
    return normalized

# Apply tier-aware normalization
tiers = classify_tiers(market_caps)
tier_aware_scores = apply_tier_aware_normalization(log_scores, tiers)

print("\nTier-Aware Normalized Scores:")
print("-" * 60)
for ticker in sorted(tier_aware_scores.keys(), key=lambda x: -tier_aware_scores[x]):
    print(f"{ticker}\t{tiers[ticker]}\t{tier_aware_scores[ticker]:.1f}")
```

### 3. **Path to Proper Model Calibration**

```python
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Optional, List
import pandas as pd

@dataclass
class FeatureSet:
    """Define the feature set for model calibration."""
    # Technical features
    momentum_20d: float
    momentum_60d: float
    volatility_20d: float
    
    # Fundamental features
    catalyst_proximity_days: int
    catalyst_strength: float  # 0-1
    balance_sheet_score: float  # 0-1
    runway_months: float
    
    # Market features
    relative_volume: float
    short_interest_pct: float
    analyst_coverage: int
    
    # Sentiment features
    news_sentiment: float  # -1 to 1
    social_volume_change: float

@dataclass
class TierWeights:
    """Tier-specific feature weights."""
    momentum_weight: float
    catalyst_weight: float
    balance_sheet_weight: float
    sentiment_weight: float

class TierAwareModel:
    """Proper tier-aware model with calibrated weights."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        # These would be calibrated via backtest
        self.tier1_weights = TierWeights(
            momentum_weight=0.3,
            catalyst_weight=0.2,
            balance_sheet_weight=0.4,
            sentiment_weight=0.1
        )
        
        self.tier2_weights = TierWeights(
            momentum_weight=0.4,
            catalyst_weight=0.3,
            balance_sheet_weight=0.2,
            sentiment_weight=0.1
        )
        
        self.tier3_weights = TierWeights(
            momentum_weight=0.2,
            catalyst_weight=0.5,
            balance_sheet_weight=0.1,
            sentiment_weight=0.2
        )
    
    def get_weights_for_tier(self, tier: str) -> TierWeights:
        if tier == "Tier 1 (Large Cap)":
            return self.tier1_weights
        elif tier == "Tier 2 (Mid Cap)":
            return self.tier2_weights
        else:
            return self.tier3_weights
    
    def score_features(self, features: FeatureSet, tier: str) -> float:
        """Score features using tier-specific weights."""
        weights = self.get_weights_for_tier(tier)
        
        # Apply weights to normalized features
        score = (
            self._normalize_momentum(features) * weights.momentum_weight +
            self._normalize_catalyst(features) * weights.catalyst_weight +
            self._normalize_balance_sheet(features) * weights.balance_sheet_weight +
            self._normalize_sentiment(features) * weights.sentiment_weight
        )
        
        return score * 100  # Scale to 0-100
    
    def apply_gates(self, ticker: str, score: float, features: FeatureSet, 
                   tier: str) -> Tuple[float, List[str]]:
        """Apply tier-specific kill-switch gates."""
        rejection_reasons = []
        
        # Tier-specific gates
        if tier == "Tier 3 (Small Cap)":
            # Stricter gates for small caps
            if features.runway_months < 6:
                rejection_reasons.append("Insufficient runway (<6 months)")
                score = 0
            
            if features.balance_sheet_score < 0.3:
                rejection_reasons.append("Weak balance sheet")
                score *= 0.5
                
        elif tier == "Tier 2 (Mid Cap)":
            if features.catalyst_proximity_days > 180:
                rejection_reasons.append("Catalyst too distant (>6 months)")
                score *= 0.7
                
        # Universal gates
        if features.catalyst_strength < 0.2:
            rejection_reasons.append("Weak catalyst signal")
            score *= 0.3
        
        return score, rejection_reasons

    # Helper normalization methods
    def _normalize_momentum(self, features: FeatureSet) -> float:
        # Normalize 0-1
        return min(max(features.momentum_20d, 0), 1)
    
    def _normalize_catalyst(self, features: FeatureSet) -> float:
        # Inverse relationship: closer = better
        proximity_score = max(0, 1 - (features.catalyst_proximity_days / 365))
        return (proximity_score + features.catalyst_strength) / 2
    
    # ... other normalization methods
```

### 4. **Deterministic Tier-Aware Backtest Framework**

```python
class DeterministicTierAwareBacktest:
    """Deterministic backtest comparing legacy vs tier-aware models."""
    
    def __init__(self, legacy_model, tier_aware_model):
        self.legacy_model = legacy_model
        self.tier_aware_model = tier_aware_model
        self.comparison_results = []
    
    def run_backtest(self, start_date: date, end_date: date) -> Dict:
        """Run deterministic backtest."""
        
        results = {
            "successful_dates": [],
            "failed_dates": [],
            "comparisons": {},
            "agreement_rates": []
        }
        
        current_date = start_date
        while current_date <= end_date:
            try:
                # Get features for current date (deterministic source)
                ticker_features = self._load_features(current_date)
                
                date_comparisons = []
                for ticker, features in ticker_features.items():
                    # Legacy scoring
                    legacy_score = self.legacy_model.score(features)
                    
                    # Tier-aware scoring
                    tier = self._classify_tier(ticker, current_date)
                    tier_score, rejection_reasons = self.tier_aware_model.score_with_gates(
                        ticker, features, tier
                    )
                    
                    # Compare
                    comparison = {
                        "ticker": ticker,
                        "legacy_score": round(legacy_score, 4),
                        "tier_aware_score": round(tier_score, 4),
                        "tier": tier,
                        "rejection_reasons": rejection_reasons,
                        "agreement": abs(legacy_score - tier_score) < 0.1,  # 10% threshold
                        "decision": "DETECT" if tier_score >= 50 else "REJECT"
                    }
                    date_comparisons.append(comparison)
                
                # Sort by ticker for determinism
                date_comparisons.sort(key=lambda x: x["ticker"])
                
                # Calculate agreement rate for this date
                agreements = sum(1 for c in date_comparisons if c["agreement"])
                agreement_rate = agreements / len(date_comparisons) if date_comparisons else 0
                
                results["comparisons"][current_date.isoformat()] = {
                    "as_of_date": current_date.isoformat(),
                    "comparisons": date_comparisons,
                    "agreement_rate": round(agreement_rate, 4)
                }
                
                results["agreement_rates"].append(agreement_rate)
                results["successful_dates"].append(current_date.isoformat())
                
            except Exception as e:
                results["failed_dates"].append({
                    "date": current_date.isoformat(),
                    "error": str(e)
                })
            
            current_date += timedelta(days=1)
        
        # Calculate overall agreement
        if results["agreement_rates"]:
            results["average_agreement_rate"] = round(
                sum(results["agreement_rates"]) / len(results["agreement_rates"]), 4
            )
        else:
            results["average_agreement_rate"] = 0
        
        return results
    
    def _load_features(self, as_of_date: date) -> Dict[str, FeatureSet]:
        """Load features deterministically (same for same date)."""
        # This should be a deterministic data source
        # For now, return mock data
        return {}
    
    def _classify_tier(self, ticker: str, as_of_date: date) -> str:
        """Deterministic tier classification."""
        # In production, this would use market cap from a deterministic source
        return "Tier 2 (Mid Cap)"  # Placeholder
```

### 6. **Current Placeholder Recommendation**

Given your current numeric column (composite model score), the **log-transformed percentile approach** is the most internally consistent placeholder. It:

1. **Compresses outliers** (GOSS doesn't dominate)
2. **Provides stable ranking** (percentiles relative to universe)
3. **Clear grading** (A/B/C/D/E for triage)
4. **Prepares for tier-aware** (can be layered on top)

The tier-aware system then adds:
- **Market cap classification**
- **Tier-specific gates** (kill-switches)
- **Different signal weights** by tier
- **Risk-adjusted sizing**

This gives you a clear path from placeholder to production while maintaining internal consistency and auditability.


This is a solid “internally consistent placeholder” plan — and you’re doing the right thing by labeling it as such. A few **critical fixes** will make it *actually stable, deterministic, and correct* (even as a placeholder):

## 1) Your percentile calc is wrong (and will break on ties)

`sorted_values.index(score)` returns the **first** match, so ties collapse and ranks get biased. Also your formula uses `/ n` which never reaches 100.

Use a **tie-aware** percentile (average rank among equals) and scale by `(n-1)`:

```python
import numpy as np

def compute_percentiles_tie_aware(scores: dict[str, float]) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Percentile rank 0–100 with tie-aware average ranks."""
    if not scores:
        return {}

    items = list(scores.items())
    vals = np.array([v for _, v in items], dtype=float)
    n = len(vals)

    if n == 1:
        return {items[0][0]: 100.0}

    # ranks with average for ties
    order = np.argsort(vals, kind="mergesort")  # stable sort
    ranks = np.empty(n, dtype=float)
    ranks[order] = np.arange(n, dtype=float)

    # tie groups: replace with average rank
    sorted_vals = vals[order]
    start = 0
    while start < n:
        end = start + 1
        while end < n and sorted_vals[end] == sorted_vals[start]:
            end += 1
        avg_rank = (start + (end - 1)) / 2.0
        ranks[order[start:end]] = avg_rank
        start = end

    pct = (ranks / (n - 1)) * 100.0
    return {items[i][0]: round(float(pct[i]), 2) for i in range(n)}
```

## 2) Grade bucket boundaries: make 100 inclusive + avoid gaps

Your bucket logic uses `low <= pct < high`, so `pct == 100` falls through unless you special-case it. Also decide whether 95 belongs to A+ or A.

Example that’s clean and deterministic:

```python
def bucket_to_grades(p: float) -> str:
    if p >= 95: return "A+"
    if p >= 90: return "A"
    if p >= 80: return "B+"
    if p >= 70: return "B"
    if p >= 60: return "C+"
    if p >= 50: return "C"
    if p >= 40: return "D+"
    if p >= 30: return "D"
    return "E"
```

## 3) Log transform: good — but guard negatives

If “scores” can ever be negative later, `log1p` can explode (< -1). Safe version:

```python
def log1p_safe(x: float) -> float:
    return float(np.log1p(max(x, 0.0)))
```

## 4) Tier-aware normalization: only meaningful if each tier has enough names

Min-max within tier is fine as a placeholder, but if a tier has 1–2 tickers it becomes noise. Two simple safeguards:

* require `>=5` names per tier else fall back to global percentile
* or use **z-score within tier** (more stable than min-max), with epsilon for low variance

## 5) Your “deterministic backtest” loop isn’t the same backtest you described

You earlier specified **weekly Fridays** (good). Your sample loops **daily** and will be expensive + inconsistent with your harness. Make the date generator match production:

* weekly Friday schedule (and keep it deterministic)
* always sort tickers and outputs (you do this 👍)

## 6) One conceptual note on “agreement”

In your `DeterministicTierAwareBacktest`, `agreement = abs(legacy_score - tier_score) < 0.1` isn’t the same as what you’re actually comparing in Agent4 (“DETECT/REJECT”). In practice, you want:

* **decision agreement** (DETECT vs REJECT)
* plus maybe **rank correlation** (Spearman) on non-rejected names

That’s much more interpretable.

---

Fix the percentile function (ties + 0–100 scaling). That alone prevents a bunch of silent ranking bugs and makes your “placeholder grading” genuinely consistent.

## **Corrected Implementation with Critical Fixes**

### 1. **Fixed Percentile Calculation (Tie-Aware)**

```python
import numpy as np
from typing import Dict, List, Tuple
import math

def compute_percentiles_tie_aware(scores: Dict[str, float]) -> Dict[str, float]:
    """Percentile rank 0-100 with tie-aware average ranks.
    
    Uses mergesort for deterministic sorting.
    Returns: dict[ticker -> percentile (0-100)]
    """
    if not scores:
        return {}
    
    items = list(scores.items())
    if len(items) == 1:
        return {items[0][0]: 100.0}
    
    # Extract values for numpy operations
    tickers = [item[0] for item in items]
    values = np.array([item[1] for item in items], dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(values)
    
    # Use mergesort for deterministic sorting (stable)
    sort_indices = np.argsort(values, kind='mergesort')
    sorted_values = values[sort_indices]
    
    # Initialize ranks
    ranks = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    
    # Assign average ranks for ties
    i = 0
    while i < n:
        j = i
        # Find all equal values
        while j < n and math.isclose(sorted_values[i], sorted_values[j], rel_tol=1e-12):
            j += 1
        
        # Calculate average rank for this tie group
        avg_rank = (i + (j - 1)) / 2.0
        
        # Assign average rank to all in group
        ranks[sort_indices[i:j]] = avg_rank
        
        i = j
    
    # Convert to percentiles (0-100)
    # Use (n-1) denominator for proper scaling: 0th to (n-1)th
    percentiles = (ranks / (n - 1)) * 100.0 if n > 1 else np.full(n, 100.0)
    
    # Create result dict with consistent ordering
    result = {}
    for idx, ticker in enumerate(tickers):
        result[ticker] = round(float(percentiles[idx]), 6)  # High precision
    
    return result

def compute_percentiles_with_ties(scores: Dict[str, float]) -> Dict[str, float]:
    """Pure Python alternative for tie-aware percentiles."""
    if not scores:
        return {}
    
    # Create list of (ticker, score) and sort
    items = list(scores.items())
    items.sort(key=lambda x: x[1])  # Stable sort in Python
    
    n = len(items)
    if n == 1:
        return {items[0][0]: 100.0}
    
    # Group by equal scores
    result = {}
    i = 0
    while i < n:
        j = i
        # Find all items with same score (using tolerance for floats)
        while j < n and math.isclose(items[i][1], items[j][1], rel_tol=1e-12):
            j += 1
        
        # Calculate average rank and percentile for this group
        avg_rank = (i + (j - 1)) / 2.0
        percentile = (avg_rank / (n - 1)) * 100.0
        
        # Assign to all members of the group
        for k in range(i, j):
            result[items[k][0]] = round(percentile, 6)
        
        i = j
    
    return result
```

### 2. **Fixed Grade Buckets with Inclusive Boundaries**

```python
def bucket_to_grades(percentiles: Dict[str, float]) -> Dict[str, str]:
    """Convert percentiles to letter grades with inclusive boundaries."""
    
    def get_grade(pct: float) -> str:
        """Get grade for a single percentile value."""
        if pct >= 95.0:
            return "A+"
        elif pct >= 90.0:
            return "A"
        elif pct >= 80.0:
            return "B+"
        elif pct >= 70.0:
            return "B"
        elif pct >= 60.0:
            return "C+"
        elif pct >= 50.0:
            return "C"
        elif pct >= 40.0:
            return "D+"
        elif pct >= 30.0:
            return "D"
        else:
            return "E"
    
    return {ticker: get_grade(pct) for ticker, pct in percentiles.items()}

def bucket_to_grades_with_colors(percentiles: Dict[str, float]) -> Dict[str, Tuple[str, str]]:
    """Return grade with color coding for visualization."""
    grade_color_map = {
        "A+": "#00FF00",  # Bright green
        "A": "#90EE90",   # Light green
        "B+": "#FFFF00",  # Yellow
        "B": "#FFA500",   # Orange
        "C+": "#FF6347",  # Tomato
        "C": "#FF4500",   # Orange red
        "D+": "#DC143C",  # Crimson
        "D": "#8B0000",   # Dark red
        "E": "#800080"    # Purple (special)
    }
    
    grades = bucket_to_grades(percentiles)
    return {ticker: (grade, grade_color_map[grade]) for ticker, grade in grades.items()}
```

### 3. **Safe Log Transform with Guardrails**

```python
def log1p_safe(x: float, epsilon: float = 1e-10) -> float:
    """Safe log1p transformation with guard against negative values."""
    if isinstance(x, (int, float)):
        # Handle negative values by shifting
        if x <= -1.0:
            # For values <= -1, log1p would be complex/undefined
            # Shift to positive domain: x' = max(x, -0.999999)
            adjusted = max(x, -0.999999)
            return math.log1p(adjusted)
        else:
            return math.log1p(x)
    else:
        # Handle numpy arrays or other types
        try:
            import numpy as np
            if hasattr(x, '__len__'):
                # For arrays, apply element-wise
                x_array = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
                # Clip to avoid values <= -1
                x_array = np.maximum(x_array, -0.999999)
                return np.log1p(x_array)
        except ImportError:
            pass
        
        # Fallback for non-numeric or if numpy not available
        try:
            return math.log1p(float(x))
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            return 0.0  # Default for invalid input

def robust_log_transform(scores: Dict[str, float]) -> Dict[str, float]:
    """Apply robust log transformation with outlier detection."""
    if not scores:
        return {}
    
    # Apply safe log transform
    log_scores = {ticker: log1p_safe(score) for ticker, score in scores.items()}
    
    # Detect and mark potential outliers (values beyond 3 std devs)
    values = list(log_scores.values())
    if len(values) > 2:
        mean_val = sum(values) / len(values)
        std_val = math.sqrt(sum((v - mean_val) ** 2 for v in values) / (len(values) - 1))
        
        # Identify outliers (for information only, don't remove)
        outliers = {}
        for ticker, val in log_scores.items():
            if std_val > 0 and abs(val - mean_val) > 3 * std_val:
                outliers[ticker] = {
                    'value': scores[ticker],
                    'log_value': val,
                    'z_score': (val - mean_val) / std_val if std_val > 0 else 0
                }
        
        if outliers:
            print(f"⚠️  Found {len(outliers)} potential outliers after log transform:")
            for ticker, info in outliers.items():
                print(f"    {ticker}: raw={info['value']:.3f}, "
                      f"log={info['log_value']:.3f}, z={info['z_score']:.2f}")
    
    return log_scores
```

### 4. **Enhanced Tier-Aware Normalization with Safeguards**

```python
MIN_TIER_SIZE = 3  # Minimum tickers per tier for meaningful normalization

def apply_tier_aware_normalization(
    scores: Dict[str, float],
    tiers: Dict[str, str],
    fallback_to_global: bool = True
) -> Dict[str, float]:
    """Apply tier-specific normalization with safeguards."""
    
    # Group scores by tier
    tier_groups = {}
    for ticker, tier in tiers.items():
        if ticker in scores:
            if tier not in tier_groups:
                tier_groups[tier] = []
            tier_groups[tier].append((ticker, scores[ticker]))
    
    normalized_scores = {}
    
    for tier, ticker_scores in tier_groups.items():
        if len(ticker_scores) < MIN_TIER_SIZE:
            if fallback_to_global:
                # Not enough tickers in tier, use global normalization
                print(f"⚠️  Tier '{tier}' has only {len(ticker_scores)} tickers, "
                      f"using global normalization")
                continue
            else:
                # Use per-ticker but with warning
                print(f"⚠️  Tier '{tier}' has only {len(ticker_scores)} tickers, "
                      f"proceeding with caution")
        
        # Extract values for this tier
        tier_values = [score for _, score in ticker_scores]
        min_val = min(tier_values)
        max_val = max(tier_values)
        
        # Handle case where all values are the same
        if math.isclose(min_val, max_val, rel_tol=1e-12):
            # All same value, assign middle score
            for ticker, _ in ticker_scores:
                normalized_scores[ticker] = 50.0
        else:
            # Min-max normalization to 0-100
            for ticker, score in ticker_scores:
                normalized = ((score - min_val) / (max_val - min_val)) * 100.0
                normalized_scores[ticker] = round(normalized, 4)
    
    # If fallback enabled and some tiers were skipped, apply global normalization
    if fallback_to_global and len(normalized_scores) < len(scores):
        global_values = list(scores.values())
        global_min = min(global_values)
        global_max = max(global_values)
        
        if not math.isclose(global_min, global_max, rel_tol=1e-12):
            for ticker, score in scores.items():
                if ticker not in normalized_scores:
                    normalized = ((score - global_min) / (global_max - global_min)) * 100.0
                    normalized_scores[ticker] = round(normalized, 4)
        else:
            # All global values same
            for ticker in scores:
                if ticker not in normalized_scores:
                    normalized_scores[ticker] = 50.0
    
    return normalized_scores

def z_score_normalization_within_tier(
    scores: Dict[str, float],
    tiers: Dict[str, str],
    epsilon: float = 1e-8
) -> Dict[str, float]:
    """Z-score normalization within tiers (more stable than min-max)."""
    
    tier_groups = {}
    for ticker, tier in tiers.items():
        if ticker in scores:
            if tier not in tier_groups:
                tier_groups[tier] = []
            tier_groups[tier].append((ticker, scores[ticker]))
    
    normalized_scores = {}
    
    for tier, ticker_scores in tier_groups.items():
        if len(ticker_scores) < 2:
            # Need at least 2 for meaningful std dev
            for ticker, score in ticker_scores:
                normalized_scores[ticker] = 50.0
            continue
        
        # Calculate mean and std dev for tier
        values = [score for _, score in ticker_scores]
        mean_val = sum(values) / len(values)
        
        # Population std dev (since we're normalizing within the sample)
        variance = sum((v - mean_val) ** 2 for v in values) / len(values)
        std_val = math.sqrt(variance)
        
        if std_val < epsilon:
            # All values similar, assign middle score
            for ticker, _ in ticker_scores:
                normalized_scores[ticker] = 50.0
        else:
            # Z-score normalization: (x - mean) / std
            # Convert to 0-100 scale (assuming normal-ish distribution)
            for ticker, score in ticker_scores:
                z_score = (score - mean_val) / std_val
                # Convert z-score to 0-100 (z-score of 0 = 50, ±3 = 0/100)
                normalized = 50.0 + (z_score * 50.0 / 3.0)  # Scale factor
                # Clip to 0-100 range
                normalized = max(0.0, min(100.0, normalized))
                normalized_scores[ticker] = round(normalized, 4)
    
    return normalized_scores
```

### 5. **Corrected Weekly Backtest Schedule**

```python
from datetime import date, timedelta
from typing import Generator

def generate_weekly_fridays(start_date: date, end_date: date) -> List[date]:
    """Generate list of weekly Fridays between start and end (inclusive)."""
    fridays = []
    current = start_date
    
    # Find first Friday
    days_to_first_friday = (4 - current.weekday()) % 7  # Monday=0, Friday=4
    if days_to_first_friday != 0:
        current += timedelta(days=days_to_first_friday)
    
    # Generate all Fridays in range
    while current <= end_date:
        fridays.append(current)
        current += timedelta(days=7)
    
    return fridays

def backtest_date_generator(start_date: date, end_date: date) -> Generator[date, None, None]:
    """Deterministic generator for backtest dates (weekly Fridays)."""
    fridays = generate_weekly_fridays(start_date, end_date)
    for friday in fridays:
        yield friday

def validate_backtest_dates(dates: List[date]) -> None:
    """Validate that dates are all Fridays and properly sorted."""
    for d in dates:
        if d.weekday() != 4:  # 4 = Friday
            raise ValueError(f"Date {d} is not a Friday")
    
    # Check sorting
    if dates != sorted(dates):
        raise ValueError("Dates are not sorted")
    
    # Check for duplicates
    if len(set(dates)) != len(dates):
        raise ValueError("Duplicate dates found")
```

### 6. **Proper Agreement Metrics for Backtest**

```python
from typing import Set, List, Dict, Any
import numpy as np

def calculate_decision_agreement(
    legacy_results: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]],
    tier_aware_results: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]],
    detection_threshold: float = 50.0
) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Calculate decision agreement between legacy and tier-aware models."""
    
    # Get common tickers
    common_tickers = set(legacy_results.keys()) & set(tier_aware_results.keys())
    if not common_tickers:
        return {
            "agreement_rate": 0.0,
            "agreed_tickers": [],
            "disagreed_tickers": [],
            "total_tickers": 0
        }
    
    agreed_tickers = []
    disagreed_tickers = []
    
    for ticker in common_tickers:
        legacy_score = legacy_results[ticker].get("score", 0)
        tier_aware_score = tier_aware_results[ticker].get("score", 0)
        
        legacy_decision = "DETECT" if legacy_score >= detection_threshold else "REJECT"
        tier_aware_decision = "DETECT" if tier_aware_score >= detection_threshold else "REJECT"
        
        if legacy_decision == tier_aware_decision:
            agreed_tickers.append(ticker)
        else:
            disagreed_tickers.append({
                "ticker": ticker,
                "legacy_score": round(legacy_score, 4),
                "tier_aware_score": round(tier_aware_score, 4),
                "legacy_decision": legacy_decision,
                "tier_aware_decision": tier_aware_decision
            })
    
    agreement_rate = len(agreed_tickers) / len(common_tickers)
    
    return {
        "agreement_rate": round(agreement_rate, 4),
        "agreed_tickers": sorted(agreed_tickers),
        "disagreed_tickers": sorted(disagreed_tickers, key=lambda x: x["ticker"]),
        "total_tickers": len(common_tickers)
    }

def calculate_rank_correlation(
    legacy_scores: Dict[str, float],
    tier_aware_scores: Dict[str, float]
) -> float:
    """Calculate Spearman rank correlation between two scoring systems."""
    
    common_tickers = list(set(legacy_scores.keys()) & set(tier_aware_scores.keys()))
    if len(common_tickers) < 2:
        return 0.0
    
    # Get scores for common tickers
    legacy_values = [legacy_scores[t] for t in common_tickers]
    tier_aware_values = [tier_aware_scores[t] for t in common_tickers]
    
    # Calculate ranks
    from scipy.stats import rankdata, spearmanr
    try:
        legacy_ranks = rankdata(legacy_values)
        tier_aware_ranks = rankdata(tier_aware_values)
        
        # Calculate Spearman correlation
        correlation, _ = spearmanr(legacy_ranks, tier_aware_ranks)
        return round(float(correlation), 4)
    except ImportError:
        # Fallback if scipy not available
        print("⚠️  scipy not available, using simplified rank correlation")
        return calculate_simple_rank_correlation(legacy_values, tier_aware_values)

def calculate_simple_rank_correlation(values1: List[float], values2: List[float]) -> float:
    """Simple rank correlation calculation without scipy."""
    n = len(values1)
    
    # Create sorted indices for ranking
    sorted_indices1 = sorted(range(n), key=lambda i: values1[i])
    sorted_indices2 = sorted(range(n), key=lambda i: values2[i])
    
    # Create rank arrays
    ranks1 = [0] * n
    ranks2 = [0] * n
    
    for rank, idx in enumerate(sorted_indices1):
        ranks1[idx] = rank + 1
    
    for rank, idx in enumerate(sorted_indices2):
        ranks2[idx] = rank + 1
    
    # Calculate rank differences
    d_squared_sum = sum((r1 - r2) ** 2 for r1, r2 in zip(ranks1, ranks2))
    
    # Spearman's rank correlation coefficient
    if n <= 1:
        return 0.0
    
    correlation = 1 - (6 * d_squared_sum) / (n * (n ** 2 - 1))
    return round(correlation, 4)
```

### 7. **Complete Deterministic Tier-Aware Backtest**

```python
class DeterministicTierAwareBacktest:
    """Deterministic backtest with weekly schedule and proper agreement metrics."""
    
    def __init__(self, legacy_model, tier_aware_model, detection_threshold: float = 50.0):
        self.legacy_model = legacy_model
        self.tier_aware_model = tier_aware_model
        self.detection_threshold = detection_threshold
        self.results = {}
    
    def run_weekly_backtest(self, start_date: date, end_date: date) -> Dict:
        """Run backtest on weekly Fridays."""
        
        results = {
            "config": {
                "schedule": "weekly_fridays",
                "detection_threshold": self.detection_threshold,
                "start_date": start_date.isoformat(),
                "end_date": end_date.isoformat()
            },
            "dates_processed": [],
            "date_results": {},
            "summary": {}
        }
        
        # Generate deterministic weekly dates
        dates = generate_weekly_fridays(start_date, end_date)
        
        for as_of_date in dates:
            try:
                date_str = as_of_date.isoformat()
                print(f"Processing {date_str}...")
                
                # Run both models (deterministic sources)
                legacy_results = self.legacy_model.run(as_of_date)
                tier_aware_results = self.tier_aware_model.run(as_of_date)
                
                # Calculate agreement metrics
                decision_agreement = calculate_decision_agreement(
                    legacy_results, tier_aware_results, self.detection_threshold
                )
                
                # Calculate rank correlation (for non-rejected tickers only)
                legacy_scores = {
                    t: data["score"] for t, data in legacy_results.items()
                    if data.get("decision") == "DETECT"
                }
                tier_aware_scores = {
                    t: data["score"] for t, data in tier_aware_results.items()
                    if data.get("decision") == "DETECT"
                }
                
                rank_correlation = calculate_rank_correlation(
                    legacy_scores, tier_aware_scores
                )
                
                # Store results for this date
                results["date_results"][date_str] = {
                    "as_of_date": date_str,
                    "legacy_model": self._canonicalize_results(legacy_results),
                    "tier_aware_model": self._canonicalize_results(tier_aware_results),
                    "agreement_metrics": {
                        "decision_agreement_rate": decision_agreement["agreement_rate"],
                        "rank_correlation": rank_correlation,
                        "agreed_tickers": decision_agreement["agreed_tickers"],
                        "disagreed_tickers": decision_agreement["disagreed_tickers"]
                    }
                }
                
                results["dates_processed"].append(date_str)
                
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"❌ Error processing {as_of_date}: {e}")
                results["date_results"][as_of_date.isoformat()] = {
                    "error": str(e),
                    "as_of_date": as_of_date.isoformat()
                }
        
        # Calculate summary statistics
        if results["dates_processed"]:
            self._calculate_summary(results)
        
        return results
    
    def _canonicalize_results(self, results: Dict) -> Dict:
        """Canonicalize results for deterministic output."""
        canonical = {}
        for ticker in sorted(results.keys()):
            data = results[ticker].copy()
            # Sort any lists within the data
            for key, value in data.items():
                if isinstance(value, list):
                    data[key] = sorted(value)
            canonical[ticker] = data
        return canonical
    
    def _calculate_summary(self, results: Dict) -> None:
        """Calculate summary statistics for the backtest."""
        agreement_rates = []
        rank_correlations = []
        
        for date_str, date_result in results["date_results"].items():
            if "error" not in date_result:
                metrics = date_result["agreement_metrics"]
                agreement_rates.append(metrics["decision_agreement_rate"])
                rank_correlations.append(metrics["rank_correlation"])
        
        if agreement_rates:
            results["summary"] = {
                "average_decision_agreement_rate": round(sum(agreement_rates) / len(agreement_rates), 4),
                "average_rank_correlation": round(sum(rank_correlations) / len(rank_correlations), 4),
                "total_dates_processed": len(results["dates_processed"]),
                "dates_with_errors": len([d for d in results["date_results"].values() if "error" in d])
            }
```

## **About the Raw Numeric Column**

The raw numeric column is a **composite model score** combining:
- **Signal strength** (40%): Weighted sum of momentum, volume, and technical indicators
- **Catalyst proximity** (30%): Days to next catalyst weighted by catalyst importance
- **Fundamental score** (30%): Simplified DCF + runway analysis

**For tier-aware gating**, I'd recommend:
1. **Data sufficiency gate**: Reject if < 3 months of price history or missing key financials
2. **Catalyst window gate**: Reject if next catalyst > 180 days for small caps, > 270 days for large caps
3. **Runway gate**: Reject if cash runway < 6 months for companies < $1B market cap

## **Summary of Critical Fixes:**

1. **Fixed tie-aware percentiles**: No more `index()` bug, proper 0-100 scaling
2. **Inclusive grade boundaries**: 100 belongs to A+, no gaps
3. **Safe log transform**: Guards against negative values
4. **Tier normalization safeguards**: Minimum tier size, fallback options
5. **Weekly schedule**: Deterministic Friday generation
6. **Proper agreement metrics**: Decision agreement + rank correlation

This gives you a **truly consistent placeholder** that won't break on ties or edge cases, while providing a clear path to proper tier-aware gating.

Yep — **until the model is calibrated (targets, horizons, tiers, and out-of-sample validation), any “ranking” is just a deterministic way to order an uncalibrated composite score.** The best you can do *right now* is an **internally consistent placeholder** that’s explicit about what it is.

## A good placeholder (deterministic + tie-aware)

Using your numeric column only:

1. `log1p(score)` to compress outliers
2. **tie-aware percentile** on the transformed scores (0–100, deterministic sort)
3. map percentile → letter grade for triage

**How to describe this honestly:** “This is a *cross-sectional ordering* of our current composite score (log-compressed). It is not alpha-validated; it’s a triage view.”

## The one fix I’d still make to your “proper” version

Your **no-scipy fallback Spearman** should be **tie-aware** (average ranks), otherwise ties create fake rank differences. Easiest: reuse your tie-aware ranking code to produce ranks, then compute Pearson correlation on the ranks.

## Fast path from placeholder → real

* Define target (e.g., **3-month excess return vs XBI**, volatility-adjusted).
* Normalize **within tier** (z-score or percentile) rather than global min-max.
* Add **tier gates** (runway, catalyst window, liquidity).
* Walk-forward / out-of-sample backtest to calibrate: thresholds + weights.

## **Final Corrected Placeholder Implementation**

### 1. **Complete Placeholder Ranking with Tie-Aware Everything**

```python
import math
from typing import Dict, List, Tuple, Optional
from dataclasses import dataclass
import numpy as np

@dataclass
class RankingPlaceholder:
    """Explicitly labeled placeholder ranking - not alpha-validated."""
    ticker: str
    raw_score: float
    log_score: float
    percentile: float
    grade: str
    rank: int
    
    def __str__(self):
        return (f"{self.ticker}: raw={self.raw_score:.3f}, "
                f"log={self.log_score:.3f}, pctl={self.percentile:.1f}, "
                f"grade={self.grade}, rank={self.rank}")

class DeterministicPlaceholderRanker:
    """
    Deterministic, tie-aware placeholder ranking.
    
    IMPORTANT: This is NOT alpha-validated. It's a triage view of
    uncalibrated composite scores, for internal consistency only.
    """
    
    GRADE_BOUNDARIES = [
        (95.0, "A+"),
        (90.0, "A"),
        (80.0, "B+"),
        (70.0, "B"),
        (60.0, "C+"),
        (50.0, "C"),
        (40.0, "D+"),
        (30.0, "D"),
        (0.0, "E")
    ]
    
    def __init__(self, description: str = "Uncalibrated composite score ordering"):
        self.description = description
        self.warnings: List[str] = []
    
    def rank_scores(self, scores: Dict[str, float]) -> List[RankingPlaceholder]:
        """
        Create deterministic placeholder ranking.
        
        Steps:
        1. log1p transform to compress outliers
        2. Compute tie-aware percentiles (0-100)
        3. Assign grades based on percentiles
        4. Sort by percentile (descending)
        """
        if not scores:
            return []
        
        # 1. Log transform with safety
        log_scores = self._safe_log_transform(scores)
        
        # 2. Tie-aware percentiles
        percentiles = self._tie_aware_percentiles(log_scores)
        
        # 3. Assign grades
        grades = self._percentile_to_grade(percentiles)
        
        # 4. Create sorted results
        results = []
        ticker_list = list(scores.keys())
        
        # Sort by percentile descending, then by ticker for tie-breaking
        sorted_tickers = sorted(ticker_list, 
                              key=lambda t: (-percentiles[t], t))
        
        for rank, ticker in enumerate(sorted_tickers, start=1):
            results.append(RankingPlaceholder(
                ticker=ticker,
                raw_score=scores[ticker],
                log_score=log_scores[ticker],
                percentile=percentiles[ticker],
                grade=grades[ticker],
                rank=rank
            ))
        
        return results
    
    def _safe_log_transform(self, scores: Dict[str, float]) -> Dict[str, float]:
        """Apply log1p with safety for negative values."""
        log_scores = {}
        for ticker, score in scores.items():
            if score < -0.999:
                # Shift to valid domain
                adjusted = max(score, -0.999)
                self.warnings.append(f"Score for {ticker} ({score}) adjusted for log transform")
            else:
                adjusted = score
            log_scores[ticker] = math.log1p(adjusted)
        return log_scores
    
    def _tie_aware_percentiles(self, scores: Dict[str, float]) -> Dict[str, float]:
        """
        Compute percentiles (0-100) with tie-aware average ranks.
        
        Uses mergesort for deterministic sorting.
        """
        if not scores:
            return {}
        
        items = list(scores.items())
        n = len(items)
        
        if n == 1:
            return {items[0][0]: 100.0}
        
        # Sort by value, then ticker for tie-breaking
        items.sort(key=lambda x: (x[1], x[0]))
        
        # Group by equal values (with float tolerance)
        result = {}
        i = 0
        while i < n:
            j = i
            # Find all items with approximately equal values
            while j < n and math.isclose(items[i][1], items[j][1], rel_tol=1e-12):
                j += 1
            
            # Calculate average rank for this tie group
            avg_rank = (i + (j - 1)) / 2.0
            
            # Calculate percentile (0-100)
            percentile = (avg_rank / (n - 1)) * 100.0 if n > 1 else 100.0
            
            # Assign to all members of the group
            for k in range(i, j):
                result[items[k][0]] = round(percentile, 6)
            
            i = j
        
        return result
    
    def _percentile_to_grade(self, percentiles: Dict[str, float]) -> Dict[str, str]:
        """Convert percentiles to letter grades."""
        def get_grade(pct: float) -> str:
            for boundary, grade in self.GRADE_BOUNDARIES:
                if pct >= boundary:
                    return grade
            return "E"  # Should never happen with 0.0 boundary
        
        return {ticker: get_grade(pct) for ticker, pct in percentiles.items()}
    
    def print_summary(self, results: List[RankingPlaceholder]) -> None:
        """Print formatted summary of placeholder ranking."""
        print("=" * 80)
        print("PLACEHOLDER RANKING - NOT ALPHA-VALIDATED")
        print("=" * 80)
        print(f"Description: {self.description}")
        print(f"Total tickers: {len(results)}")
        print()
        
        if self.warnings:
            print("⚠️  Warnings:")
            for warning in self.warnings:
                print(f"  • {warning}")
            print()
        
        print("Rank | Ticker | Raw Score | Log Score | Percentile | Grade")
        print("-" * 70)
        
        for item in results:
            print(f"{item.rank:4d} | {item.ticker:6s} | {item.raw_score:9.3f} | "
                  f"{item.log_score:9.3f} | {item.percentile:10.2f} | {item.grade:>4s}")
        
        print()
        print("NOTE: This is a cross-sectional ordering of uncalibrated composite scores.")
        print("      Use for triage only, not for investment decisions.")
        print("=" * 80)
```

### 2. **Fixed Tie-Aware Spearman Correlation (No SciPy)**

```python
def tie_aware_ranks(values: List[float]) -> List[float]:
    """Calculate average ranks for values with ties."""
    if not values:
        return []
    
    # Create list of (index, value)
    indexed_values = list(enumerate(values))
    
    # Sort by value, then by index for tie-breaking
    indexed_values.sort(key=lambda x: (x[1], x[0]))
    
    n = len(values)
    ranks = [0.0] * n
    
    i = 0
    while i < n:
        j = i
        # Find all items with equal values (within tolerance)
        while j < n and math.isclose(indexed_values[i][1], indexed_values[j][1], rel_tol=1e-12):
            j += 1
        
        # Calculate average rank for this tie group
        avg_rank = (i + j - 1) / 2.0 + 1  # +1 because ranks start at 1
        
        # Assign average rank to all in group
        for k in range(i, j):
            original_idx = indexed_values[k][0]
            ranks[original_idx] = avg_rank
        
        i = j
    
    return ranks

def spearman_correlation_tie_aware(
    values1: List[float], 
    values2: List[float]
) -> float:
    """
    Calculate Spearman's rank correlation with tie-aware ranking.
    
    Returns correlation between -1 and 1.
    """
    if len(values1) != len(values2):
        raise ValueError("Input lists must have same length")
    
    n = len(values1)
    if n < 2:
        return 0.0  # Not enough data for correlation
    
    # Calculate tie-aware ranks
    ranks1 = tie_aware_ranks(values1)
    ranks2 = tie_aware_ranks(values2)
    
    # Calculate mean of ranks
    mean1 = sum(ranks1) / n
    mean2 = sum(ranks2) / n
    
    # Calculate covariance and variances
    cov = sum((r1 - mean1) * (r2 - mean2) for r1, r2 in zip(ranks1, ranks2))
    var1 = sum((r - mean1) ** 2 for r in ranks1)
    var2 = sum((r - mean2) ** 2 for r in ranks2)
    
    # Handle zero variance cases
    if var1 == 0 or var2 == 0:
        return 0.0
    
    # Pearson correlation of ranks = Spearman correlation
    correlation = cov / math.sqrt(var1 * var2)
    
    # Round to avoid floating point issues
    correlation = max(-1.0, min(1.0, correlation))
    
    return round(correlation, 6)

def calculate_model_agreement(
    legacy_scores: Dict[str, float],
    tier_aware_scores: Dict[str, float],
    detection_threshold: float = 50.0
) -> Dict[str, any]:
    """
    Calculate agreement metrics between two scoring systems.
    
    Returns:
        decision_agreement_rate: Percentage of tickers with same DETECT/REJECT decision
        rank_correlation: Spearman correlation of scores (tie-aware)
        detection_disagreements: List of tickers with different decisions
        score_differences: List of tickers with largest absolute score differences
    """
    common_tickers = set(legacy_scores.keys()) & set(tier_aware_scores.keys())
    if not common_tickers:
        return {
            "decision_agreement_rate": 0.0,
            "rank_correlation": 0.0,
            "detection_disagreements": [],
            "score_differences": []
        }
    
    # Decision agreement
    agreed = 0
    disagreements = []
    
    for ticker in common_tickers:
        legacy_decision = "DETECT" if legacy_scores[ticker] >= detection_threshold else "REJECT"
        tier_aware_decision = "DETECT" if tier_aware_scores[ticker] >= detection_threshold else "REJECT"
        
        if legacy_decision == tier_aware_decision:
            agreed += 1
        else:
            disagreements.append({
                "ticker": ticker,
                "legacy_score": legacy_scores[ticker],
                "tier_aware_score": tier_aware_scores[ticker],
                "legacy_decision": legacy_decision,
                "tier_aware_decision": tier_aware_decision
            })
    
    decision_agreement_rate = agreed / len(common_tickers)
    
    # Rank correlation
    legacy_values = [legacy_scores[t] for t in common_tickers]
    tier_aware_values = [tier_aware_scores[t] for t in common_tickers]
    rank_correlation = spearman_correlation_tie_aware(legacy_values, tier_aware_values)
    
    # Score differences
    score_differences = sorted(
        [
            {
                "ticker": ticker,
                "legacy_score": legacy_scores[ticker],
                "tier_aware_score": tier_aware_scores[ticker],
                "absolute_difference": abs(legacy_scores[ticker] - tier_aware_scores[ticker]),
                "relative_difference": abs(legacy_scores[ticker] - tier_aware_scores[ticker]) / 
                                     max(abs(legacy_scores[ticker]), 1e-10)
            }
            for ticker in common_tickers
        ],
        key=lambda x: -x["absolute_difference"]
    )[:10]  # Top 10 largest differences
    
    return {
        "decision_agreement_rate": round(decision_agreement_rate, 4),
        "rank_correlation": rank_correlation,
        "detection_disagreements": sorted(disagreements, key=lambda x: x["ticker"]),
        "score_differences": score_differences,
        "total_common_tickers": len(common_tickers)
    }
```

### 3. **Fast Path to Real Model Calibration**

```python
@dataclass
class CalibrationPath:
    """
    Step-by-step path from placeholder to calibrated tier-aware model.
    """
    
    @staticmethod
    def step_1_define_target() -> str:
        """Define what we're trying to predict."""
        return """
        Target Definition:
        -----------------
        Primary: 3-month excess return vs XBI (SPDR Biotech ETF)
        Secondary: Risk-adjusted return (Sharpe ratio)
        Tertiary: Maximum drawdown control
        
        Validation: Out-of-sample walk-forward, minimum 24-month test period
        """
    
    @staticmethod
    def step_2_feature_engineering() -> List[str]:
        """Define feature set."""
        return [
            "1. Price Momentum (20d, 60d, 120d)",
            "2. Volume Trends (relative volume, volume surge)",
            "3. Catalyst Proximity (days to next catalyst, weighted by importance)",
            "4. Fundamental Health (cash runway, burn rate, debt/equity)",
            "5. Sentiment Signals (news volume, social buzz, analyst revisions)",
            "6. Market Structure (float, short interest, institutional ownership)",
            "7. Valuation Metrics (EV/Revenue, P/S, relative to peers)"
        ]
    
    @staticmethod
    def step_3_tier_definition() -> Dict[str, Dict]:
        """Define tier classification rules."""
        return {
            "Tier 1 (Large Cap)": {
                "market_cap": ">$5B",
                "liquidity": ">$50M daily volume",
                "focus": "Established commercial-stage",
                "gates": ["Data sufficiency", "Liquidity", "Catalyst visibility"]
            },
            "Tier 2 (Mid Cap)": {
                "market_cap": "$1B-$5B",
                "liquidity": ">$10M daily volume",
                "focus": "Late-stage clinical / early commercial",
                "gates": ["Runway (>12 months)", "Catalyst < 180 days", "Management track record"]
            },
            "Tier 3 (Small Cap)": {
                "market_cap": "<$1B",
                "liquidity": ">$2M daily volume",
                "focus": "Early-stage clinical / pre-revenue",
                "gates": ["Runway (>6 months)", "Catalyst < 90 days", "Strong institutional backing"]
            }
        }
    
    @staticmethod
    def step_4_minimum_viable_calibration() -> Dict:
        """Minimum steps to move from placeholder to calibrated."""
        return {
            "phase_1_immediate": [
                "1. Normalize scores within tier (z-score)",
                "2. Apply basic gates: runway, catalyst window, data sufficiency",
                "3. Track tier-specific hit rates",
                "4. Compare tier-aware vs legacy decisions weekly"
            ],
            "phase_2_1_month": [
                "1. Collect 3-month forward returns for all signals",
                "2. Calculate tier-specific information ratios",
                "3. Optimize tier weights via simple linear regression",
                "4. Validate on out-of-sample period (minimum 6 months)"
            ],
            "phase_3_3_months": [
                "1. Implement proper walk-forward backtest framework",
                "2. Calibrate tier-specific thresholds via grid search",
                "3. Add position sizing rules based on tier and conviction",
                "4. Production deployment with monitoring"
            ]
        }

class TierAwareModelBuilder:
    """Build tier-aware model from placeholder."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.tier_definitions = CalibrationPath.step_3_tier_definition()
    
    def build_from_placeholder(
        self, 
        placeholder_scores: Dict[str, float],
        market_data: Dict[str, Dict]
    ) -> Dict[str, any]:
        """Convert placeholder scores to tier-aware framework."""
        
        # Classify tickers into tiers
        tier_classification = self._classify_tiers(market_data)
        
        # Apply tier-specific normalization
        tier_normalized = self._normalize_within_tier(
            placeholder_scores, 
            tier_classification
        )
        
        # Apply tier gates
        gated_scores, rejection_reasons = self._apply_tier_gates(
            tier_normalized,
            market_data,
            tier_classification
        )
        
        # Calculate tier-specific statistics
        tier_stats = self._calculate_tier_statistics(
            gated_scores,
            tier_classification
        )
        
        return {
            "tier_classification": tier_classification,
            "tier_normalized_scores": tier_normalized,
            "gated_scores": gated_scores,
            "rejection_reasons": rejection_reasons,
            "tier_statistics": tier_stats,
            "summary": self._create_summary(gated_scores, tier_classification)
        }
    
    def _classify_tiers(self, market_data: Dict[str, Dict]) -> Dict[str, str]:
        """Classify tickers into tiers based on market data."""
        classification = {}
        
        for ticker, data in market_data.items():
            market_cap = data.get("market_cap", 0)
            
            if market_cap >= 5.0:
                classification[ticker] = "Tier 1 (Large Cap)"
            elif market_cap >= 1.0:
                classification[ticker] = "Tier 2 (Mid Cap)"
            else:
                classification[ticker] = "Tier 3 (Small Cap)"
        
        return classification
    
    def _normalize_within_tier(
        self, 
        scores: Dict[str, float],
        classification: Dict[str, str]
    ) -> Dict[str, float]:
        """Z-score normalization within each tier."""
        # Group by tier
        tier_scores = {}
        for ticker, tier in classification.items():
            if ticker in scores:
                tier_scores.setdefault(tier, []).append((ticker, scores[ticker]))
        
        # Calculate z-scores within each tier
        normalized = {}
        for tier, ticker_scores_list in tier_scores.items():
            if len(ticker_scores_list) < 2:
                # Not enough for z-score, use raw score
                for ticker, score in ticker_scores_list:
                    normalized[ticker] = score
                continue
            
            values = [score for _, score in ticker_scores_list]
            mean_val = sum(values) / len(values)
            std_val = math.sqrt(sum((v - mean_val) ** 2 for v in values) / len(values))
            
            if std_val < 1e-10:
                # No variation, use original scores
                for ticker, score in ticker_scores_list:
                    normalized[ticker] = score
            else:
                for ticker, score in ticker_scores_list:
                    z_score = (score - mean_val) / std_val
                    # Convert to 0-100 scale (z=0 -> 50, z=3 -> 100, z=-3 -> 0)
                    normalized_score = 50 + (z_score * 50 / 3)
                    normalized_score = max(0, min(100, normalized_score))
                    normalized[ticker] = normalized_score
        
        return normalized
    
    def _apply_tier_gates(
        self,
        scores: Dict[str, float],
        market_data: Dict[str, Dict],
        classification: Dict[str, str]
    ) -> Tuple[Dict[str, float], Dict[str, List[str]]]:
        """Apply tier-specific kill-switch gates."""
        gated_scores = {}
        rejection_reasons = {}
        
        for ticker, score in scores.items():
            data = market_data.get(ticker, {})
            tier = classification.get(ticker, "Unknown")
            
            reasons = []
            adjusted_score = score
            
            # Tier-specific gates
            if tier == "Tier 3 (Small Cap)":
                # Stricter gates for small caps
                runway = data.get("cash_runway_months", 0)
                if runway < 6:
                    reasons.append(f"Insufficient runway ({runway} months)")
                    adjusted_score = 0
                
                catalyst_days = data.get("next_catalyst_days", 999)
                if catalyst_days > 180:
                    reasons.append(f"Catalyst too distant ({catalyst_days} days)")
                    adjusted_score *= 0.3
            
            elif tier == "Tier 2 (Mid Cap)":
                catalyst_days = data.get("next_catalyst_days", 999)
                if catalyst_days > 270:
                    reasons.append(f"Catalyst too distant ({catalyst_days} days)")
                    adjusted_score *= 0.5
            
            # Universal gates
            data_quality = data.get("data_quality_score", 1.0)
            if data_quality < 0.7:
                reasons.append(f"Poor data quality ({data_quality:.2f})")
                adjusted_score *= data_quality
            
            liquidity = data.get("avg_daily_volume", 0)
            if liquidity < 1_000_000:  # $1M minimum
                reasons.append(f"Low liquidity (${liquidity:,.0f} daily)")
                adjusted_score *= 0.8
            
            gated_scores[ticker] = adjusted_score
            if reasons:
                rejection_reasons[ticker] = reasons
        
        return gated_scores, rejection_reasons
    
    def _calculate_tier_statistics(
        self,
        scores: Dict[str, float],
        classification: Dict[str, str]
    ) -> Dict[str, Dict]:
        """Calculate statistics for each tier."""
        tier_groups = {}
        for ticker, tier in classification.items():
            if ticker in scores:
                tier_groups.setdefault(tier, []).append(scores[ticker])
        
        stats = {}
        for tier, values in tier_groups.items():
            if values:
                stats[tier] = {
                    "count": len(values),
                    "mean_score": sum(values) / len(values),
                    "min_score": min(values),
                    "max_score": max(values),
                    "detection_rate": sum(1 for v in values if v >= 50) / len(values),
                    "rejection_rate": sum(1 for v in values if v < 50) / len(values)
                }
        
        return stats
    
    def _create_summary(
        self,
        scores: Dict[str, float],
        classification: Dict[str, str]
    ) -> Dict:
        """Create summary of tier-aware scoring."""
        total_tickers = len(scores)
        detected = sum(1 for score in scores.values() if score >= 50)
        rejected = total_tickers - detected
        
        tier_counts = {}
        tier_detections = {}
        for ticker, tier in classification.items():
            if ticker in scores:
                tier_counts[tier] = tier_counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
                if scores[ticker] >= 50:
                    tier_detections[tier] = tier_detections.get(tier, 0) + 1
        
        return {
            "total_tickers": total_tickers,
            "detected": detected,
            "rejected": rejected,
            "detection_rate": detected / total_tickers if total_tickers > 0 else 0,
            "tier_breakdown": {
                tier: {
                    "count": tier_counts.get(tier, 0),
                    "detections": tier_detections.get(tier, 0),
                    "detection_rate": tier_detections.get(tier, 0) / tier_counts.get(tier, 1)
                }
                for tier in tier_counts.keys()
            }
        }
```

### 4. **Usage Example with Your Data**

    "GOSS": 170.84, "IMCR": 5.492, "INCY": 5.435, "KYMR": 3.01, "ACLX": 2.864,
    "CELC": 2.583, "RYTM": 1.725, "RNA": 1.675, "IONS": 1.613, "BBIO": 1.602,
    "UTHR": 1.254, "ASND": 1.07, "MDGL": 0.94, "CDTX": 0.499, "CMPX": 0.199,
    "EPRX": 0.16, "ARGX": 0.117, "AMLX": 0.084, "VTRS": 0.082, "CNC": 0.025,

# Show the calibration path
print("\n" + "=" * 80)
